# IDEA REGISTER

Every retained candidate lives here with a complete dossier. Status values: `shortlisted`,
`candidate` (retained but not shortlisted), `archived` (retained for reference, not a top
opportunity), `rejected` (see `rejected_and_parked.md`; dossiers are not maintained for those).

Last updated: **Cycle 59 complete** (Cycle 59 executed the register's remaining highest-value
*state-availability* questions and **settled all of them, without creating a candidate and without changing
a score**. (i) **G-28 (IDEA-004)** was re-checked against the *structural* question — is there a published
**pair-level, netted** position source, i.e. one that publishes a participant's longs *and* shorts, or a net
position, at a granularity at which a two-legged crowd is visible? — and the answer is **no, for a
structural reason**: every published position-disclosure source is **security-level and single-sided**
(13F is long-only and aggregated; 13D/13G are beneficial-ownership disclosures; FINRA short interest is an
issue-level aggregate with no firm-level breakdown; CFTC category tables are aggregates), while a
convergence crowd is defined by a **pair** of positions, so no such source can represent it. **V-002 stays
frozen**, and the next agent must not re-run the old, search-based question. (ii) **G-21 (IDEA-011)** was
**handed off to validation rather than re-searched**: the first-time-versus-second-time-adopter drift
comparison is not merely unrun but **unused**, because the field's largest comparability study (S-294) is a
first-time-adopter design *by construction* and does not inventory drift, and the nearest published outcome
family (S-295) is likewise first-time-adopter — so the comparison would be a **contribution, not a
replication**, and the claim must be pre-registered as a **transition-window** effect. (iii) **G-30
(IDEA-010)** was **confirmed exhausted** across all four routes outside the statutory prospectus, with
**no re-probe**; **V-010 stays non-executable** against the corporate-bond ETF universe, and no published
crowding proxy may be substituted for the AP set. (iv) **IDEA-014's state-free existence limb** was
maintained **inconclusive** — no effort without a new source, and **no gap opened** on it. (v) **G-32 limb
(c)** and **G-33's re-check** were recorded as **future obligations, dated 2027 Q3**; the G-33 sample read
must **not** be run again from scratch, because the six interim reports have now been read (S-281 … S-286;
**5 of 6 line-verified, 6 of 6 index-confirmed**, the Saudi Tadawul leg index-rendered behind an Akamai
layer that returns HTTP 403 on every route), the quantified expected effect was **0 of 6** — so the
re-check is about *comparability across firms*, not *quantification* — and none of the six is framed as an
IAS 8.30–31 disclosure but as a *not-yet-effective* / *recent-accounting-developments* note, so the re-check
should look for the same **kind** of note in the 2027 tranche. (vi) Cycle 59 ran the register's **second
state-first family screen** — the **published price of a constraint** (repo specialness, securities-lending
fee curves, CDS financing spreads, standing-repo award rates) — logged as a **bounded negative (R-039)** on
five independent reasons; **no `S-` identifier was consumed**, and the screen's decision line and its
known-baseline row were written at the wrap-up. **The Cycle-59 wrap-up was completed in a later session**
and changed **no verdict, no gap closure and no score**: it wrote the R-039 decision line and the missing
known-baseline row (now **68 rows**), added the Cycle-59 record to the register, the shortlist and the
validation file, de-duplicated the cycle log's two Cycle-59 entries, and corrected the stale
next-free-identifier pointers that still advertised R-039 and Cycle 59 after R-039 had been consumed.
Earlier history: **Cycle 58 complete** (Cycle 58 executed the register's two highest-value *state-availability*

own conditioning variable (a dealer's FVA is computed from its own funding curve, S-010/S-155, so a
funding-cost proxy "predicting" it is an accounting identity, not a measure of dealer-specific

multi-year**, so the specified validation sample is not constructible; and the state the candidate
actually needs is **dealer-specific** while every public ingredient is market-wide, so the conditioning
would change only the *size* of an effect whose sign and existence are already published (S-009, S-292,

No published primary-source route to a corporate-bond ETF's AP set exists outside the statutory prospectus.) **G-29 (IDEA-010) — executed as a calibrated document-level search.** The register's trade-press lead was verified from the primary document (**S-239**: the *iShares Bitcoin Trust ETF* Form 424B3 prospectuses name their authorised participants verbatim — five APs in January 2024, nine in April, twelve by August 2024 and **twelve cash plus four in-kind APs as of 31 July 2025**), so the detector used to search the population is known to work. The detector sentence returns **five documents in four filers across all prospectus form types, every one of them a crypto or commodity trust, and no corporate-bond ETF**; the complementary phrase-first route over 591 bond-worded filings and the name-first route over bond-worded filings containing any of thirty-one AP firm names produce the same answer (the AP names occur only in securities-lending ownership tables and the standing 25 %-record-holder disclosure). Screened **(A) the eligibility switch** (*who may hold*, published compliance date, published thresholds) on the SEC's accredited-investor and QIB definition amendment (**S-244**) and on index-provider eligibility and float screens — **bounded negative (R-020)**. **No score changed; no candidate created; no new validation specification added.**), 2026-10-20. *The narrative below still describes the state as of Cycle 13; Cycles 14–29 were monitoring passes that changed nothing, and Cycles 30–40's own changes are recorded in each dossier's blocks and in `cycle_notes.md`.*
register was empty before Cycle 1; Cycle 2 added three dossiers outside listed US equities.
**Closure passes: eight candidates have now been demoted — seven by their own literature and one by a
publication calendar** (IDEA-002 joined the "by its own literature" group in Cycle 58: it was rejected on
its own dossier's prediction 4 and its own parking entry's re-open condition, with P-004 closed). IDEA-007 was
narrowed to v1.1 after the funding-currency corpus was read — its aggregate version is a published
baseline (S-055, rejected as R-006) and the dossier then claimed only the cross-sectional and
publication-timing residual — and in Cycle 5 that residual was closed against it as well: the
cross-section is a published, positive result (S-064) and the mechanism's literature has closed in
the same direction (S-095, S-096, S-097), so IDEA-007 is **rejected (R-008)**. IDEA-006 was narrowed
to v1.1 after the intraday fixing literature was read — the fix-window price impact is published
(S-074 … S-081, rejected as R-007) — and in Cycle 5 its own gate was met: **G-11 closed against it,
so IDEA-006 is rejected (R-009)** and its dedicated vehicle IDEA-012 is **rejected (R-010)**.
**Cycle-3 gap closures:** G-10 cross-sectional residual remains open; G-11 partially closed —
settlement-side mechanics are a priced friction (S-082, S-071) but the deadline cluster is untested;
G-06 narrowed — the commodity basis-convergence literature (S-032 … S-039) does not condition on
passive share × depth, so IDEA-005's state-conditioned differentiator survives as a conjecture.
**Cycle-5 gap closures (this cycle):** **G-10 closed against IDEA-007** (see the R-008 record);
**G-11 closed against IDEA-006 and IDEA-012** (a settlement deadline cluster is a published price
determinant — S-112; the securities settlement cycle is a published price-impact determinant —
S-113, S-114, S-115; the post-US-close FX window is documented as a cost rather than an edge —
S-117, S-118, S-119, S-120); **G-06 closed as a bounded negative finding** with the papers that had
not been found earlier now located and registered (S-098 conditions spot–futures convergence on
*trader type*; S-099 conditions *commodity returns during roll periods* on the CIT market share and
reports no significant effect — contradicting evidence now recorded against IDEA-005); **G-08 mostly
closed** and **G-05 split** (its single-name 0DTE series is constructible from public OCC data, so
the *data* question is closed and only the *literature* question is open); **G-12 opened** — the
general question of whether a *published* state can change the *sign or existence* of a published
effect, or only its measurement quality, which is now the register's standing test for every
state-conditioned candidate; and **G-13 opened** — a published state is usable as a decision-time
variable only if it is published *before* the decision, an established instance being the EU ETS
aggregate balance (S-121), which was published after the pre-2023 surrender deadline it was meant to
inform. **IDEA-013 was parked (P-008)** after its required full-text check: S-101 does not condition
the premium on the aggregate surplus (so the candidate is not a re-description of published work),
but the published model already implies the conditional structure, the published sample contains a
high-surplus year in which the effect persisted, and the decision-time form of the candidate has
only three observations.
**Cycle-6 gap closures and screens (this cycle).** The two standing general questions were applied
as **screening conditions to every active candidate**: **G-13** (is the state published *before* the
decision?) and **G-12** (does the conditioning change the *sign or the existence* of the effect,
rather than only its size?). Every candidate's dossier below now carries an explicit
**"Cycle 6 screen"** block recording both verdicts and what they do to the dossier. Two candidates
were **parked** — **IDEA-005 (P-009)** because the closest published test of its own state variable
is null (S-099) and the family's post-2012 aggregate result is null (S-033), so its conditioning
cannot be shown to change the sign or the existence of the effect; and **IDEA-008 (P-010)** because
its own named state — securities-lending fees and availability — is not publicly disseminated
before **29 March 2029** (S-126), so it fails G-13 on a dated, institutional ground rather than a
judgement. **One candidate was re-scored — IDEA-010, 68.0 → 65.0** (its mechanism's core is published
institutionally, S-123) — and four were re-checked with **no score change**: IDEA-001, whose G-12
narrowing is priced into the existing novelty score, IDEA-011, IDEA-003 and IDEA-009. **One new candidate was
written up: IDEA-014 RIN-DEADLINE** (the US Renewable Fuel Standard's compliance-deadline
obligation), the only one of the five frontier families that survived the screen with an unclaimed
mechanism *and* a state that is published before the decision (the obligation's size is codified at
40 CFR 80.1405 — S-128; the deadline is published by regulation — S-129; the deadline has already
been moved once by published rule — S-131). Its sign is **not** asserted: the sign is its first
empirical test (G-15), which is why it enters at 60.0 and not higher.

**Cycle 13 (this cycle) — one rejection, two re-scores, four gap closures, six frontier screens.**
The cycle's centre of gravity was the published 2024–2026 literature on index reconstitution, which
the register had read only through 2023. **IDEA-001 RECON-FLOW, the rank-1 candidate, is REJECTED
(R-011)**: S-146 (*Journal of Finance* 2025) documents that the S&P 500 index effect has collapsed
— additions below 1% over the past decade, about −3 basis points in 2020, deletions 0.1% between
2010 and 2020 — and that there is "no apparent relationship between net purchases [by index
trackers] and the index inclusion effect", which is a published null against the candidate's stated
differentiator; S-147 (Pegoraro, Sammon & Shim, working paper) models the mechanism as *anticipatory
liquidity provision* that shifts price pressure away from the effective date, with short interest
rising into implementation and falling afterwards, which contradicts the candidate's causal chain;
and S-151 explains why: the counterparty to passive demand is the issuing firm, so there is no
arbitrageur paying a concession to harvest. Two gaps closed *in the candidate's favour* before it
died — **G-03** (the effective-close convention is documented by the index manager itself, S-149,
and by peer-reviewed work, S-150) and **G-02/G-07** (a published method for estimating
benchmark-specific tracked assets now exists, S-148) — which is the point of running the gap work
and the screen work in the same cycle: the candidate was killed on evidence, not on a
missing-premise technicality. **Two re-scores:** IDEA-004 74.0 → 69.0 (published, mechanistically
explained decay of the momentum-family base effect, S-156, plus a bounded negative on
crowding-conditioned average returns, S-172) and IDEA-009 63.0 → 60.0 (the funding-rate mechanism
and its mean-reversion are now published independently, S-158, S-159). **One gap closed against a
variant:** **G-05's** literature half, where single-name variance risk premia are published as
frequently zero or of the opposite sign to the index premium (S-153, S-154) — the aggregate version
of IDEA-003 survives untouched. **One gap reopened in part:** **G-04**, because a decision-time,
public, price-computable crowding proxy now exists (S-157, "comomentum"), which opens **G-18**
(validate it against a holdings-based crowding measure) rather than licensing a re-specification.
**Six frontier families screened, no new candidate:** crypto token unlocks (documented, S-170),
California Cap-and-Invest (documented *and* practically inert, S-163/S-164/S-174), the Treasury
General Account → repo channel (published, S-160–S-162), accelerated share repurchases (published,
S-166, S-167), the California reserve-sale supply valve (a published, state-triggered, date-bound
supply event that has never been used), and the perpetual-funding family (published, S-158, S-159).
**No new dossier was written in Cycle 13** — the honest outcome, and the third consecutive cycle in
which the screen produced rejections and closures rather than candidates. The register now holds six
active dossiers (IDEA-003, IDEA-004, IDEA-009, IDEA-010, IDEA-011, IDEA-014), of which three are
shortlisted.

**Cycle 30 (this cycle) — three evidence operations and one family screen, no score change.** After
twelve consecutive monitoring passes (Cycles 18–29) that changed nothing, this cycle did what the
register's own cross-cutting note identifies as its highest-value act — re-read existing candidates
against literature the register had not yet seen — and screened one new mechanism family.
**IDEA-003 (71.0, unchanged):** state-conditioned variance-premium harvesting is a **published
strategy family** (S-180) and the premium-as-tail-premium framing is published in the candidate's own
vocabulary (S-181), so the candidate is reclassified as an **incremental variant of published
conditional-VRP work**; more importantly, the two published constructions of the premium use
**different measurement conventions** (S-180 closest to the expected-premium convention; S-181
ex-post), so the candidate's "the premium collapses" prediction is now written convention-explicitly,
**V-003 is amended** (an ex-post null is *not* a kill) and **G-19** is opened. **IDEA-004 (69.0,
unchanged):** the newest peer-reviewed 13F-crowding work (S-186) shows the holdings-based construction
is an active published object but associates crowding with prices, comovement and **fragility** rather
than with a return-predictive rule, so **G-18 is restated, not closed** and **V-002 stays frozen**.
**IDEA-014 (60.0, unchanged):** the deadline's movement is **rule-determined** and the *realised*
sign-determining state publishes only *after* the deadline it informs (S-184, S-185), so **G-15 is
sharpened** and **G-20** opened. **One new family screened; one new dossier written, parked: IDEA-015
PLAN-SUPPLY (61.0, P-011, G-21)** — pre-announced, non-discretionary insider supply whose state is
published by **corporate disclosure law** (S-182, S-183), parked because the nearest published price
effect attaches to plan *adoption* around earnings rather than to the execution of a disclosed
schedule. **One bounded negative: R-012** (the statutory payment-date liquidity-drain family). The
register now holds six active dossiers (three shortlisted), **five** parked and four rejected, and
**fifteen** dossiers in total.

**Cycle 35 (this cycle) — one executed closure that parks a candidate, one closure in a candidate's
favour, one closure that supersedes its own premise, one narrowing, two bounded negatives, and a
state-integrity repair.** The cycle began by auditing the register against itself, and the audit
found that the Cycle-33 source-ledger header and the Cycle-34 handoff asserted a set of artifacts —
the sources S-210 … S-218, the "Cycle 33 sources" and "Cycle 33 gap verdicts" blocks, the candidate
**IDEA-016 RGGI-COMPLIANCE**, the specification V-011, the bounded negative R-014, the parkings P-012
and P-013, and a formal G-25 verdict — that **have never existed in any file in this repository**; no
commit in the git history has ever contained any of them, and the "Cycle 33 execution" note that
claimed otherwise was itself part of the false header. Cycle 35 corrected the header, **executed** the
two claimed closures that were research questions rather than documents, wrote the claimed screen for
real, and recorded the reserved identifiers as reserved so that none of them can ever be reused.
**IDEA-003 (66.0, score unchanged, now parked):** G-22 was executed rather than assumed. The published
option-implied crash literature forecasts crash events, predicts the expected market return from a
crash measure (S-223) and prices crash risk; none of it asks whether a crash state improves the
risk-adjusted value of harvesting variance, and S-223's measure is a hybrid of an option-implied
variance and *realised* moments rather than a pure option-surface measure. By the rule G-22 registered
in Cycle 32, the candidate is a known baseline with an unvalidated state overlay and **must be parked
(P-012)** — parked, not rejected, because nothing published shows the candidate's effect.
**IDEA-010 (68.0, unchanged):** G-23 closed **in the candidate's favour**. The capital state is
quarterly and lagged (S-225, S-226), but the claim never required the state to change *inside* the
holding window, so the identification is re-anchored to a cross-sectional contrast (weak-capital
versus strong-capital ETFs on redemption days), the 1–10 session horizon is preserved, the signedness
becomes the first pre-registered killable test with the three rivals applied to the *interaction*, and
the effective sample and the per-AP filing comparability are registered as G-27. **IDEA-004 (69.0,
unchanged):** G-18 closed as a bounded negative that supersedes its own premise — the literature never
needed a comomentum-versus-holdings validation, because each construct has been validated against an
*outcome*; the newest peer-reviewed work (S-222) makes crowding a published first-order conditioning
variable with a crash-risk channel, which the candidate may no longer present as a discovery, and
makes the *state definition* the load-bearing specification, registered as G-28. **IDEA-011 (69.0,
unchanged):** G-21 narrowed to a named discriminator — the Greek case is attributed to a disclosure
shock by its own authors (S-188), "first adoption" does not predict the sign of the drift effect
(S-179 runs the other way), the published conditioner is *enforcement* (S-221), and the candidate's
diffusion clock must start before the effective date (S-220); the residual is a first-adopter versus
second-adopter drift comparison that has not been run. **IDEA-014 (52.0, unchanged):** G-24 and G-25
closed as bounded negatives for the conditional version; the claimed RGGI replacement state never
existed, and the state-free existence limb is retained. **IDEA-015 (61.0, unchanged):** P-011
confirmed — the published 10b5-1 effect attaches to adoption and participation, not to the execution
window (S-224). **Two mechanism families screened, two bounded negatives, no new dossier:** the crypto
token-unlock re-screen (R-014) and the EU CBAM certificate purchase obligation (R-016). **Eleven new
sources, S-220 … S-230. No score changed.** The register now holds five active dossiers (three
shortlisted), **six** parked and four rejected, and **fifteen** dossiers in total.

## IDEA-001 — RECON-FLOW: announcement-timed, flow-scaled reconstitution pressure with a post-effective reversal leg

- **ID / short name:** IDEA-001 / RECON-FLOW
- **Date added:** 2026-10-19 · **Cycle:** 1 · **Status:** **rejected (R-011, Cycle 13)** ·
  **Version:** 2.0 (terminal)
- **Change notes:** v1.0 — initial dossier. Effect family is documented in prior literature; the
  candidate is the *conditional, flow-scaled, two-leg specification*, not the effect itself.
  **v2.0 (Cycle 13) — REJECTED.** The 2024–2026 literature on index reconstitution (S-146, S-147,
  S-148, S-151) closes the candidate from three directions: the *base effect* has disappeared in the
  modern sample (additions below 1% over the past decade; about −3 basis points in 2020); the
  *differentiator* — flow scaling — has a published null (no apparent relationship between mechanical
  buying by index trackers and the inclusion effect); and the *mechanism* is now modelled as
  anticipatory liquidity provision that shifts price pressure away from the effective date, with
  short interest rising into implementation and falling after. The dossier is retained unaltered
  below as the historical record; the decision is in `rejected_and_parked.md` (R-011). Its
  validation specification V-001 is **withdrawn**.

**One-sentence hypothesis.** Index-provider reconstitution creates a scheduled, deadline-driven
passive demand shock that is knowable in advance, and the part of the resulting price move that
reflects the arbitrageur's compensation for providing liquidity to that shock partly reverses after
the passive flow has been executed.

**Plain-language description.** When S&P announces that a stock will join the S&P 500, index funds
must buy it at the effective close, five days later. Somebody has to sell that stock to the funds
before the deadline, and the market's price moves up to compensate the seller for the risk of
carrying the position and for the fact that their exit is crowded. After the funds have bought,
that seller unwinds and part of the price move goes back the other way.

**Mechanism (causal chain).**
1. S&P DJI fixes an announcement timestamp and an effective date; constituent changes are
   announced one to five days ahead, S&P 500 announcements are published at 05:15 ET, changes
   take effect after the close on the third Friday of the quarter-end month, and a share freeze
   applies in the preceding week (S-008).
2. Funds tracking the benchmark must trade to the new weights on the effective date; the required
   dollar flow for a given name is a deterministic function of the index weight change and the
   assets tracking the index. This is a *scheduled* demand curve shock, not an information shock.
3. The passive flow is not price-sensitive (the funds must trade at the close regardless of price),
   so the price must move to ration a finite liquidity supply. The price concession paid to the
   counterparty is the compensation.
4. A risk arbitrageur ("the S&P game", S-002) supplies the stock ahead of the effective date and
   sells into the passive demand. That is a *business* for the arbitrageur, not a free lunch.
5. The arbitrageur must eventually unwind; the unwind is itself a scheduled, correlated sale in
   the same name. If the fundamental value has not changed, part of the initial concession
   reverses (S-002, S-004).
6. The magnitude of both legs should scale with the *size of the passive flow requirement* —
   i.e. with the index weight change multiplied by the assets tracking the index — and with the
   liquidity of the affected name. This scaling is the differentiator: the classic literature
   treats index events largely as one homogeneous type.

**Expected return source.** A compensation for providing liquidity to a deadline-driven,
non-price-sensitive buyer (a structural liquidity-provision premium), plus the arbitrageur's
scheduled-unwind reversal, net of execution costs and adverse selection. This is a *flow* premium
and a *timing* premium, not a risk premium for systematic exposure.

**Originality.**
- *Closest known work:* the index-inclusion literature — Harris & Gurel (1986) [S-001], Beneish &
  Whaley (1996) [S-002], Chen/Noronha/Singal (2004) [S-003], Woglom (2002) [S-004]; option-market
  evidence [S-005, S-006, S-007]; and the "holdings effect" debate summarised in [S-004].
- *What is already known:* the announcement price increase, the announcement-to-effective drift, a
  partial post-effective reversal, the asymmetry between additions and deletions, and the fact that
  the effect grew with index-fund assets.
- *Substantive differentiator claimed here:* (i) conditioning every leg on the **size of the
  passive flow requirement** rather than treating events as homogeneous; (ii) specifying the
  **post-effective reversal as a separately tradable leg** with its own entry/exit rule rather
  than as a description of a price path; (iii) an explicit **asymmetry of who can arbitrage** —
  on additions an arbitrageur can pre-position and be paid; on deletions the index fund must sell
  into a one-sided book and no comparable arbitrageur exists — which is a flow-and-capacity
  explanation for the addition/deletion asymmetry that [S-003] attributes to investor awareness.
- *Honest classification:* **documented effect, incremental conditional refinement.** This is not
  a new effect and must never be presented as one. Its value is that it is unusually clean to
  test and to falsify, with published event dates that require no look-ahead.
- *Confidence in the comparison:* high. The classic papers are directly on point and were read
  (abstracts plus full text for S-002 and S-004).

**Target scope.** US large-cap equities (S&P 500, S&P MidCap 400, S&P SmallCap 600 additions and
deletions); event-driven holding from the announcement after-close to the effective close (leg 1)
and from the effective close to roughly 1–20 sessions after (leg 2); monthly frequency of events
(quarterly reconstitution plus intra-quarter changes). Regime conditions: intact index futures
market, low index-futures basis stress, no venue outage at the effective close.

**Observables (known at decision time, conceptually).**
- Announcement timestamp and the announced change (published; S-008).
- Effective date and the index weight change implied by the reconstitution.
- Assets tracking the benchmark (needed as a scale variable; see source gap G-02).
- The affected name's liquidity: quoted spread, depth, average daily volume around the event.
- Whether the name is an "outsider" addition (moving in from outside the S&P 1500 family) versus
  an intra-family promotion — the flow requirement differs by an order of magnitude [S-007].
- **Timing hazards to flag for the validator:** the announcement is published after the close
  (05:15 ET), so leg 1 cannot be entered at the announcement close without trading in the
  after-hours/overnight session; the effective-date close is the passive execution moment, so
  leg 2 must be entered *after* the passive flow is done (next session onwards) and its exit must
  be scheduled to avoid the crowded window; a share-freeze week and corporate actions can change
  the required weights between announcement and effective date; a name can be added and later
  removed within the same window; and index-option expiries and index-futures rolls coincide with
  the effective date, contaminating the flow.

**Rule sketch (descriptive, non-executable).** At the announcement after-close, for each announced
addition (and separately for deletions), compute the required passive flow as a function of the
weight change and the assets tracking the benchmark; screen for the affected name's liquidity and
for a non-degenerate announcement-to-effective window. Leg 1: establish a long position in the
affected name after the announcement, sized so that the position is small relative to the expected
passive flow requirement, and hold to the effective-date close. Leg 2: close or reverse the
position after the effective date over a pre-specified number of sessions. Exclusions: names
trading below a minimum dollar volume, names with a corporate action inside the window, events
inside a scheduled index-futures roll week.

**Persistence thesis.** The opportunity exists because the passive buy is not price-sensitive and
is scheduled, so the price concession is not competed away: the arbitrageur is *paid* to absorb the
flow, and payment is a cost to the index fund, not a loss to an informed counterparty. It decays if
(a) index funds shift to pre-positioning (which would remove the pre-effective leg), (b) passive
assets grow so large that the arbitrage becomes the dominant participant (which would compress the
concession and increase the risk that the arbitrageur's own exit crowds it), or (c) execution
becomes cheap enough (fractional/algorithmic closing-auction participation) that the concession
disappears. All three are observable in principle.

**Constraints.**
- *Cost/liquidity:* the affected names are large-cap and liquid, but the trade must be small
  relative to the passive flow, so the per-event dollar capacity is small; the reversal leg is
  executed in a name where the arbitrage crowd is also unwinding.
- *Capacity:* capacity is bounded by the size of the flow requirement, so the strategy scales with
  passive assets, not with the arbitrageur's balance sheet.
- *Financing/borrow:* a long-only leg 1 has no borrow requirement; a long/short reversal variant
  (short the affected name against the market) does, and the affected name is typically easy to
  borrow, but borrow can be scarce exactly in the crowded window.
- *Market impact:* the trade is small relative to the passive flow by construction, but the
  reversal leg trades *against* the same crowd.
- *Venue/operational:* a single close on a single exchange; a venue outage or a limit-up/limit-down
  halt at the effective close breaks the premise; index funds and the arbitrageur compete for the
  same closing-auction liquidity.
- *Financing of the event:* the position is held for at most a few days, so financing is a spread
  cost, not a balance-sheet constraint.

**Regime sensitivity, tail exposure, crash behavior, hidden beta.**
- Regime: the effect depends on index-fund trading convention and passive scale; it should be
  weakest when passive funds pre-position and strongest when they trade at the effective close.
- Tail exposure: leg 1 is a long position held for days with an event-driven exit; it carries
  market beta and overnight gap risk, and the announcement-to-effective window contains
  idiosyncratic news.
- Crash behavior: additions to an index performed during a crisis recovery can gap; the price
  concession and the fundamental news are confounded.
- Hidden beta: the leg is a long-only small-cap-of-index event position; sizing must control
  market beta explicitly, otherwise the "alpha" is a beta bet.
- Correlated risks: all events in a reconstitution window share the same date; a single market
  shock dominates many small event-level results.

**Falsifiable predictions and rejection conditions.**
1. *Announcement-to-effective drift:* additions drift up and deletions drift down, with magnitude
   increasing in the required passive flow. **Kill:** the drift is not monotone in flow size, or
   disappears once announcement-window abnormal options volume is controlled for (which would
   indicate the announcement is being anticipated by informed option flow rather than by
   price pressure — see [S-005]).
2. *Post-effective partial reversal:* a non-zero fraction of the leg-1 move reverses within
   1–20 sessions after the effective date, and the reversal is larger when leg 1 was larger.
   **Kill:** no reversal at all, or a reversal that is fully explained by a change in the
   fundamental news environment or by an index-option/futures roll.
3. *Flow scaling:* effect size scales with weight change × assets tracking the benchmark, not just
   with "being in the index". **Kill:** no relation to flow size.
4. *Asymmetry:* additions give a harvestable spread; deletions do not, because the passive seller
   faces a one-sided book. **Kill:** deletions show an equally large and equally harvestable move
   in the passive direction.
5. *Crowding null:* if the post-effective reversal is entirely explained by a broad reversal in
   additions, the mechanism is a common factor, not a liquidity concession.

**Main confounders and rival explanations.**
- *Informed trading ahead of the announcement:* options markets and insiders may trade the
  announcement; [S-005] finds pre-announcement abnormal options volume predicts smaller
  announcement returns. Any flow-scaling result must separate price pressure from anticipation.
- *Awareness/attention:* the addition makes the name visible; a retail attention flow (cf. [S-027])
  is an alternative explanation for the announcement response, and it would not predict the
  post-effective reversal or the flow scaling.
- *Fundamental news:* additions are not random — companies with rising market value, M&A activity
  and index eligibility change together. This is the single most important confounder.
- *Index-option and index-futures mechanics* on the effective date, and the "sell the news" of
  the arbitrageur's own unwind.
- *Survivorship in the event sample:* deleted names often fail, so the deletion leg is contaminated
  by fundamental news in a way the addition leg is not.

**Data requirements and feasibility for a future independent validation.** (Specification only —
nothing is acquired or processed here.) Public: S&P DJI announcements and index-weight changes
(S-008 gives the schedule; the provider publishes constituent and weight files), daily OHLCV and
closing-auction data, index membership history, and a series for assets tracking the benchmark
(gap G-02). Feasibility: high for the event study, medium for the flow-scaling test (depends on
G-02) and medium for the reversal leg (needs precise close-to-close and auction data).

**Theory/evidence status.**
- *Conceptual rationale:* supported by the price-pressure and liquidity-provision mechanism
  [S-001, S-002, S-004] and by the venue's own rules [S-008].
- *Mechanism supported by sources:* yes for the announcement drift; the flow-scaling
  specification and the arbitrage-capacity asymmetry are **unresolved conjectures** added here.
- *Prior empirical support reported by others:* yes, extensively [S-001, S-002, S-003, S-004,
  S-005, S-006, S-007] — reported by their authors, not reproduced here.
- *Unresolved:* whether the effect is still present at post-2024 passive scale and under modern
  closing-auction practice.

**Remaining uncertainty / confidence.** Confidence in the *core effect*: high (published,
repeatedly). Confidence in the *differentiated specification* (flow scaling + two-leg trade +
side asymmetry): moderate; the components are individually unproven. Priority score 76/100;
rank 1 of 7 (rank 1 of 4 at Cycle 1; score and rank unchanged at Cycle 2). **Cycle 13: score
withdrawn — the candidate is rejected (R-011).** **Concrete next
question:** does the announcement-to-effective drift scale with the
required passive flow (a quantitative, publicly checkable prediction), and is the post-effective
reversal statistically separable from awareness and information-leakage explanations?

**Cycle 6 screen (G-13 publication timing; G-12 sign or existence).**
- **G-13 — the state is published before the decision: PASS.** The state the rule conditions on is
  the *reconstitution announcement*, which publishes the event type, the weight change and the
  effective date, and is published before the effective close the rule trades (S-029). The event
  schedule carries no look-ahead. Two qualifications, both already on the record: the *scale* of the
  passive flow requirement (weight change × assets tracking) is not published by any institution —
  it is the product of a published index weight (S-029) and an industry estimate of tracked assets
  (S-040) — so the **flow-scaling** limb rests on an estimated, not a published, quantity; and the
  premise that a same-close auction absorbs the flow is not publicly guaranteed (G-03). Neither is
  a publication-timing defect.
- **G-12 — the conditioning changes the sign or the existence of the effect: PASS on the existence
  limb, FAIL on the size limb.** This is the screen's most useful result for this candidate. The
  **flow-scaling** claim ("the drift scales with the required passive flow") conditions the *size*
  of a published effect and would not survive G-12 by itself — it would be a baseline
  re-description with an extra regressor. The candidate survives only on two claims that do pass:
  (i) the **side asymmetry** — additions produce a harvestable concession while deletions do not, so
  the effect's *existence as a tradeable spread* differs across event types, where the published
  literature attributes the asymmetry to investor awareness rather than to flow and capacity
  (S-003); and (ii) the **existence of the second leg** — a non-zero fraction of the leg-1 move
  reverses within 1–20 sessions, which is a separately dated and separately tradeable effect rather
  than a larger version of leg 1.
- **Effect on the dossier: no score change (76.0).** The G-12 outcome is already priced — novelty
  is 2/5, the lowest in the register, because the underlying effect is forty years old and
  published. The screen makes the dossier's "differentiated specification" line precise: **the
  differentiator is the asymmetry and the second leg, not the scaling.** A validation that measured
  only the drift's magnitude would not test what the candidate claims.

**Cycle 13 screen (G-13 publication timing; G-12 sign or existence) — AND REJECTION.**
- **G-13 — the state is published before the decision: still PASS, and the two Cycle-6 qualifications
  are now resolved in the candidate's favour.** The premise that the flow is executed at the
  effective close is no longer an assumption: it is documented by the index manager itself (S-149:
  index funds and ETFs are "structurally incentivized to execute trades at the closing auction"
  because official NAVs are struck on closing prices, and Market-on-Close orders are "the dominant
  mechanism for end-of-day execution") and by the peer-reviewed literature (S-150 on closing-auction
  volume share, price impact and passive creation/redemption attribution; S-148 on execution at the
  closing price on reconstitution day). The *scale* qualification is also resolved: a **published
  construction method** for benchmark-specific tracked assets now exists (S-148 infers the AUM
  tracking each of five named indices from reconstitution-day closing volume, 2000–2021), so the
  flow-scaling limb no longer rests on an industry estimate (S-040). **G-03 and G-02/G-07 are closed
  as construction questions.**
- **G-12 — the conditioning changes the sign or the existence of the effect: FAIL, on every limb.**
  This is where the candidate dies. The published 2024–2026 evidence (S-146) states that (i) the
  index effect itself has collapsed — the S&P 500 addition abnormal return has fallen from 7.4% in
  the 1990s to **less than 1% over the past decade**, deletions average 0.1% between 2010 and 2020,
  and the average inclusion effect in 2020 was **−3 basis points** — so there is no concession of
  tradeable size left to harvest; (ii) the candidate's *differentiator* has a **published null**:
  "there is no apparent relationship between net purchases [by index trackers] and the index
  inclusion effect, for either additions or deletions"; and (iii) the *published mechanism*
  contradicts the candidate's causal chain. The candidate's chain requires the price concession to
  be paid by the passive's counterparty *between the announcement and the effective close* and to
  persist until the arbitrageur unwinds. The 2026 model (S-147) instead has speculators
  **pre-positioning before the event**, which "shifts price pressure away from the reconstitution
  date", with "apparent front-running reflect[ing] competitive liquidity provision" that "benefits
  index investors", and with short interest **rising on implementation dates and falling afterwards**
  as speculators cover — i.e. the residual documented structure is *anticipation before the event*
  and *unwind after it*, not a concession harvested in the announcement-to-effective window.
- **The side-asymmetry limb and the second leg are foreclosed too.** The asymmetry claim needed
  additions to produce a concession while deletions do not; with both effects now at approximately
  zero (−3 bps and 0.1%), the asymmetry has no economic content in the modern sample. The
  second-leg claim needed a *non-zero fraction of the leg-1 move* to reverse within 1–20 sessions —
  but the published unwind pattern (short interest falling after implementation) is the *same
  documented structure*, executed by speculators rather than by passive-flow arbitrageurs, and it is
  now a published result rather than an unclaimed residual.
- **What survives, and why it is not a candidate.** One structural fact remains genuinely useful and
  is now the register's best reading of this family: the counterparty to a passive fund's demand is
  **the issuing firm itself**, and most demand-side institutions trade *in the same direction* as
  index funds (S-151). That is the strongest published evidence *for* the persistence of passive-flow
  pressure — and it is precisely the reason the 2024–2026 literature can explain a vanished effect:
  when the seller is the firm and the pressure is anticipatory, there is no arbitrageur paying a
  concession to harvest, and the price simply does not move. The correct conclusion is that the
  *mechanism is real and the price impact has been competed away*, not that the effect is mismeasured.
- **Effect on the dossier: the candidate is rejected (R-011).** A peer-reviewed, published result
  (S-146) shows the candidate's *effect* has effectively disappeared in the modern sample, a
  published null rejects the candidate's *differentiator* (flow scaling), and a published model
  (S-147) contradicts the candidate's *mechanism and timing*. That is the register's rejection
  standard applied to a candidate whose only remaining claim was a second leg on an effect that no
  longer exists. Score withdrawn; V-001 withdrawn. The reusable outputs are filed as baselines:
  "the disappearing index effect", "anticipatory liquidity provision at reconstitution", and the
  reconstruction method of S-148.
