## IDEA-004 — SLOWCAP-COND: capacity-conditional convergence trades under slow-moving capital


- **ID / short name:** IDEA-004 / SLOWCAP-COND
- **Date added:** 2026-10-19 · **Cycle:** 1 · **Status:** shortlisted · **Version:** 1.2
- **Change notes:** v1.0 — initial dossier. The limits-to-arbitrage family is heavily studied; the
  candidate is the *observable crowd-depth state* used to condition the trade. v1.1 (Cycle 35) — G-18
  closed as a bounded negative that supersedes its own premise; G-04 restated; G-28 opened; V-002
  remains frozen for a new reason; no score change (69.0).
  **Cycle 52 — no score change (69.0), rank 2 unchanged.** G-28 was **re-derived** by a bounded check
  rather than re-declared: no published measure of *convergence-crowd position concentration* was
  located, and every crowding measure the register holds measures a different crowd, so **G-28 and V-002
  stay frozen** and the FINRA route stays a lead with objections. The R-032 screen added one structural
  point to this dossier: a state that is **broadcast in real time** (S-260, S-261) cannot be the source
  of an informational edge, so a future unfreezing of G-28 would have to show that the published measure
  is **not** a public real-time by-product of the crowd's own trading. No source bearing on any score
  component was found.
  **v1.2 — Cycle 58: G-04 CLOSED on the proxy-validity limb, in the candidate's favour, and G-28's freeze
  given a structural reason.** The register's long-standing demand for a comomentum-versus-holdings
  cross-validation is retired: every crowding construct the register holds has already been validated
  against a *published outcome* (S-026, S-222 against crash and tail risk; S-157 against arbitrage
  activity and returns; and, newly registered this cycle, S-291's position-based arbitrage-activity
  measures, S-242's short-interest arbitrage-capital measure and S-296's ownership dispersion). What
  binds the candidate instead is the **identity** limb, G-28, and Cycle 58 established a reason that does
  not depend on further searching: every published position-disclosure source is **security-level and
  single-sided**, while a convergence crowd is defined by a **pair** of positions (long one leg, short the
  other), so no such source can represent it. **V-002 stays frozen** with a sharper unfreezing condition;
  the prohibition on substituting any published crowding proxy is reaffirmed. New hard-stop flag added:
  a candidate may not argue the crowd is unmeasured merely because no measure was found. No score change
  (69.0, rank 2 unchanged).

**One-sentence hypothesis.** Because arbitrage capital is slow to arrive and exits in
synchronisation, a convergence trade's expected return and its crash risk are both functions of
how crowded the convergence crowd already is, and the crowd depth can be partially observed.

**Plain-language description.** When a stock is mispriced, the money that should fix it does not
come instantly — it comes slowly, and only if the mispricing gets deep enough. So the bigger the
gap and the thinner the crowd, the longer the gap can persist, but when the crowd is already
heavy, the same gap will be competed shut faster and everyone's exit will collide.

**Mechanism (causal chain).**
1. Equilibrium with slow-moving capital: arbitrage capital only moves when fire-sale discounts are
   deep enough; stand-by capital is limited, and contagion arises when unrelated assets draw on a
   common pool of arbitrage capital (S-017); capital is empirically and theoretically slow
   (S-018, S-019).
2. Mandates, delegation and career concerns bind capital (S-020); manager reputation can make
   capital slow sometimes and fast other times, moving prices with no fundamental news (S-021).
3. Flow-driven price pressure of exactly this kind produces momentum when flow inertia makes
   prices underreact to expected future flows, and reversal when the same flows push prices away
   from fundamental value (S-024) — the sign depends on the state of the flow process.
4. Arbitragers who fail to act in time are forced to synchronise later, raising the price of
   liquidity and amplifying deviations (S-023).
5. The strategy that trades convergence is therefore exposed to a *crowding* risk: as more capital
   runs it, the price effect that generates the profit shrinks and the risk of a simultaneous exit
   rises (S-022, S-025). Crowding itself is measurable at the security level (S-026) and is
   associated with tail risk.
6. Hence: the expected value of a convergence trade is a function of (deviation, crowd depth,
   exit capacity), not of the deviation alone. The candidate's claim is that crowd depth is
   partially observable and is a *first-order* conditioning variable, not a risk-management overlay.

**Expected return source.** Compensation for supplying capital/liquidity to slow-moving,
constraint-bound arbitrageurs and institutions, plus the slow unwinding of price pressure away
from fundamental value (S-019, S-024).

**Originality.**
- *Closest known work:* the limits-to-arbitrage literature — Shleifer & Vishny's framing, Kondor
  [S-022], Abreu & Brunnermeier [S-023], Acharya/Shin/Yorulmazer [S-017], Duffie [S-019], plus
  empirical work on risk arbitrage [S-025] and on measurable crowding [S-026].
- *What is already known:* deviations persist; arbitragers are synchronised; crowding is a risk
  factor; crowding is measurable with slow, stale data.
- *Substantive differentiator claimed here:* a *trade specification* in which the crowd-depth state
  is the gate on both entry and exit, and in which the claim is about the **direction of the
  conditioning** (crowding raises expected convergence return in the short run because capital is
  slow, and lowers it while raising tail risk in the exit) — a two-sided relation that a simple
  "avoid crowded trades" rule does not capture. The related published result [S-026] finds
  crowdedness is associated with worse tail outcomes; it does not (to my knowledge) exploit the
  slow-capital mechanism to argue for *when* to take the convergence side.
- *Honest classification:* **informed variant of a documented family with a new conditioning
  variable.** The risk is that the conditioning variable is unobservable in a timely way (G-04), in
  which case this is a re-description of a known baseline.
- *Confidence in the comparison:* moderate.

**Target scope.** Cross-sectional equity pairs (industry-relative and single-name), merger-spread
and index-convergence style relative-value where measurable; holding horizons of days to months;
regime conditions: excluded during market-wide liquidity events where the crowd exits together.

**Observables.** The deviation (a concept-dependent spread); the crowd-depth state, in principle
from: reported holdings of arbitrage and multi-strategy funds (lagging, S-026), the dispersion of
analyst targets as a proxy for disagreement crowding, the concentration of option open interest
around the trade's strikes, aggregate short interest, the count and size of recently announced
comparable trades, and the fund-flow state of the strategy complex. All must be available with
their reporting lag accounted for.

**Rule sketch (descriptive, non-executable).** Identify convergence candidates (a specified
relative-value universe with a pre-defined fundamental anchor). Compute the deviation. Compute the
crowd-depth state from lagged observable proxies, and the exit-capacity state. Take the
convergence side only when the deviation is large *relative to the crowd depth* (i.e. the gap is
not already the consensus trade), size inversely to the crowd depth (capacity of the exit), and
pre-specify an exit that is spread over time and is suspended during market-wide liquidity events.
Maintain the crowd state as a first-class input to the sizing rule, not merely as a risk limit.

**Persistence thesis.** Slow capital, mandates, and reputation-driven capital immobility are
structural features of delegated asset management (S-017, S-020, S-021) and change slowly; the
deviations they create unwind slowly (S-019, S-024). It decays if capital becomes mobile
(electronically distributed advice, retail arbitrage platforms, internalised crossing), if
crowd depth is observable and therefore arbitraged away, or if the anchor itself becomes
contestable.

**Constraints.** Cost/liquidity: convergence trades are often in less liquid names; the spread is
paid on both legs and the trade is a market-neutral pair, so the cost is doubled. Capacity: the
capacity of the trade is the capacity of the *exit*, which is shared with the crowd — the
candidate's own sizing rule handles this, but it also caps the strategy's size at exactly the
moments the signal is strongest. Financing/financing cost: carry on a market-neutral pair is a
direct drag and can exceed the convergence payoff. Borrow: short legs in crowded names are the
most expensive to borrow exactly when the trade is most attractive. Market impact: taking the
crowded side of a small-cap pair is an impact machine. Venue/operational: paired execution adds
leg-risk. Short-sale constraints: borrow recalls can force early, adverse exits.

**Regime sensitivity, tail exposure, crash behavior, hidden beta.** Regime-sensitive: the crowd
state itself is the regime. Tail: convergence trades have a classic left tail — the fundamental
anchor breaks (M&A fails, an accounting restatement) and the trade becomes a directional loss,
which is unrelated to the crowd state and therefore not hedgeable by the rule. Crash behaviour:
the crowd exits together, so the crowd state should be read as a *warning*, not merely a size
modifier. Hidden beta: convergence pairs in equities carry residual market, sector and factor
exposures; the state rule does not remove them. Correlated risks: all names in one crowded
strategy are the same trade.

**Falsifiable predictions and rejection conditions.**
1. *Conditioning direction:* convergence returns are larger when the deviation is large relative to
   crowd depth, and the relationship is non-monotone in crowd depth (higher in moderate crowding
   than in low crowding, lower in high crowding). **Kill:** the relation is monotone and
   negative throughout, in which case the candidate collapses into "avoid crowded trades" and
   should be reclassified as a known baseline.
2. *Tail asymmetry:* the left-tail severity of the strategy is higher in high-crowd states, and the
   rule's crowd gate reduces expected return but also reduces tail severity. **Kill:** no
   difference in tail severity across crowd states.
3. *Exit-capacity effect:* the reversal is faster in high-crowd states (the crowd's exit accelerates
   the convergence) but with worse gap risk. **Kill:** convergence speed is unrelated to crowd
   depth.
4. *Proxy validity:* the crowd proxy must be shown to carry information about the *actual* position
   concentration of arbitrage capital (validation against a holdings-based measure such as
   [S-026]). **Kill:** the proxy has no relation to the holdings-based crowding measure — in which
   case the candidate is not testable as specified (G-04) and must be parked.

**Main confounders and rival explanations.** Fundamental-break risk (the anchor is wrong) is the
dominant confounder and is not addressed by the crowd state; momentum is a rival explanation for
any positive convergence-return pattern (S-024) — the crowd state and momentum loadings must be
separated; liquidity premia (S-009-style) explain pairs returns without any slow-capital story;
crowding proxies are stale and may simply proxy for size, value or momentum factor exposures;
and the "crowdedness" literature already documents worse tail outcomes [S-026], so a naive
crowding-avoidance result is not evidence for this candidate.

**Data requirements and feasibility for a future independent validation.** Pair-level prices and
borrow costs; a defined universe and anchor; holdings-based crowding data [S-026] (or an
equivalent institutional holdings database) for proxy validation; optionally short interest and
option open interest. Feasibility: **medium** — the prices and the anchor are easy, the crowd
proxy is the bottleneck, and its reporting lag is a design constraint, not an implementation detail.

**Theory/evidence status.** Conceptual rationale: strongly supported [S-017, S-019, S-020, S-021,
S-023, S-024]. Mechanism supported by sources: yes for the slow-capital and synchronisation
channels; the *joint* crowd-depth conditioning of a convergence trade is an unresolved conjecture.
Prior empirical support reported by others: for crowding and tail risk [S-026]; for risk-arbitrage
characteristics [S-025]. Unresolved: the timely observability of crowd depth (G-04) and the
direction of the conditioning (prediction 1).

**Remaining uncertainty / confidence.** Confidence in the mechanism: high. Confidence in the
two-sided conditioning: moderate-to-low — this is the falsifiable core. Confidence in timely
observability: low-to-moderate. Priority score 74/100; rank 2 of 7 (rank 2 of 4 at Cycle 1; **Cycle 13:
re-scored 74.0 → 69.0 and rank 2 → 3;**
unchanged). **Concrete next question:** is
the relation between convergence returns and crowd depth non-monotone as predicted, and can a
timely proxy for crowd depth be validated against a holdings-based measure?

**Cycle 6 screen (G-13 publication timing; G-12 sign or existence).**
- **G-13 — the state is published before the decision: PASS, but only on a quarterly-anchored
  rule.** This screen **resolves a question the Cycle-5 handoff left open** for this candidate. The
  crowd state is built from institutional holdings filings, and those filings are **published**
  documents with a **statutory cadence**: a Form 13F must be filed within 45 days of the end of the
  calendar quarter and is filed to EDGAR, so the *components* of the crowd measure are public
  before the start of the following quarter. A rule anchored to a quarter boundary can therefore
  use everything filed up to its decision date. The screen's finding is narrower than it looks,
  and the dossier's "reporting lag is a design constraint" line must be read with it: the
  **daily-frequency** proxies (short interest, option open interest, analyst targets) are *not*
  decision-time crowd measures at all, and the rule cannot be run at daily frequency on a
  holdings-based state. The binding constraint on this candidate is therefore **not** timing and
  **not** publication — it is the **proxy's validity** (G-04, prediction 4). That is a different
  and, on the evidence, a better-posed question than the one the Cycle-5 draft assumed.
- **G-12 — the conditioning changes the sign or the existence of the effect: PASS, on the sign limb.**
  The candidate's own kill condition says it: if the relation is "monotone and negative
  throughout", the candidate collapses into "avoid crowded trades" and is a known baseline. A
  *non-monotone* relation means the conditioning **flips the sign** of the trade's expected return
  — moderate crowding supports the convergence trade, extreme crowding inverts it into a momentum
  trade. That is a sign claim, not a size claim, and it is the strongest form of G-12.
- **Effect on the dossier: no score change (74.0).** Both tests pass. One *design* consequence is
  recorded and must be carried into any validation specification: the decision calendar is now
  fixed at **filing dates**, so V-002's rebalancing windows must be re-anchored from any daily or
  monthly grid to the 45-day filing cadence, and the proxy must be assembled point-in-time from
  filings published up to the decision date.

**Cycle 13 screen (G-13 publication timing; G-12 sign or existence) — re-score and a new proxy
candidate.**
- **G-13 — PASS on the holdings-based state, unchanged.** Nothing in Cycle 13 changes the filing
  cadence analysis above. The 45-day Form 13F lag remains the binding constraint for the
  holdings-based measure (S-026).
- **G-12 — PASS on the sign limb, unchanged, but the base effect it conditions has decayed.** The
  candidate's sign claim (moderate crowding supports the convergence trade; extreme crowding inverts
  it into a momentum trade) is untouched. What is new is published evidence about the *base* the
  gate conditions on: the momentum factor's returns fell from 0.92% per month (January 1987 – June
  2002) to **0.16% per month** afterwards, with an identified mechanism — positive-feedback trading
  in analyst ratings ended after the mid-2002 Morningstar rating reform (S-156, *JFQA* 2023). This
  is directly material because IDEA-004's own dossier lists momentum as a *rival explanation* for its
  convergence returns: the confounder that must be separated out has itself become much weaker.
  Separately, a 2025 preprint (S-172) reports that **crowding-based factor selection fails
  out-of-sample** (Sharpe 0.22 against a 0.39 factor-momentum benchmark), that crowding predicts
  *tail risk* rather than average returns, and that cross-sectional factor timing does not work.
  Preprint weight is low and its title has already changed between versions (a concrete instance of
  version drift), but it is a bounded negative against the *average-return* branch of the crowding
  conditioning.
- **A decision-time proxy candidate has been found (this is the cycle's constructive output for
  IDEA-004).** The Cycle-8 closure of G-04 was right that no *holdings-based* timely proxy exists,
  but it did not consider *inferred* crowding measures: **comomentum** (S-157) infers arbitrage
  activity from return correlations, so it is computable from public price data at decision time with
  no filing lag. It is a *different construct* from the holdings-based position-concentration measure
  (S-026), and it has never been validated against it. The register does not permit re-specifying
  around an unvalidated proxy, so the candidate is **not** re-specified; instead **G-18 is opened**
  (see `rejected_and_parked.md` and the shortlist's gap list) and the validation specification V-002
  must remain frozen until G-18 is closed.
- **Effect on the dossier: re-scored 74.0 → 69.0 (mechanism/causal coherence/persistence 5 → 4).**
  The mechanism itself — slow-moving capital crowding into convergence trades — is not damaged, and
  the *sign* claim is intact; what weakens is **persistence**: the family whose returns the candidate
  harvests has a published, large, and mechanistically explained decline (S-156), and the published
  factor-level evidence now leans toward crowding mattering for *tail risk* rather than for average
  returns (S-172, preprint). A candidate whose base effect has halved twice over is a lower research
  priority than it was at 74.0, even though its conditional structure is unchanged. Novelty stays at
  3/5 (the non-monotone, sign-flipping gate remains unclaimed; S-157 changes the *measurement*
  question, not the novelty question), evidence stays at 4/5, and the data dimension stays at 3/5
  until G-18 is closed.

**Cycle 30 G-18 / G-04 advance — the newest holdings-based crowding work makes the state constructible
and reconfirms the bounded negative; no score change (69.0); V-002 stays frozen.**
- **What was searched, and how the question was reframed.** G-18 asks whether a decision-time, public,
  price-computable crowding measure (comomentum, S-157) is a valid **substitute** for the holdings-based
  measure this candidate is frozen on (S-026). Thirteen prior cycles answered that question by looking
  for a *head-to-head validation paper* and found none. This cycle asked a different, more useful
  question: **is there published work that constructs a decision-time, filing-resolved, holdings-based
  crowding measure, and what does the current literature say about using crowding to predict returns?**
  That framing reaches the same gap from the other side and produced an answer worth recording.
- **Finding 1 — the state is an active, published construction (S-186).** The newest peer-reviewed
  holdings-based crowding work (Hu & Shen, *Finance Research Letters*, 8 October 2026) measures **manager
  crowding directly from 13F holdings** for the largest portfolios over 2013Q2–2025Q4, and reports that
  common positions, correlated institutional trading and shared ownership are "associated with prices,
  comovement, and fragility". Two consequences for this dossier. (i) The construction that IDEA-004's
  **G-04 proxy-validity kill condition** refers to is no longer an open methodological question: a
  current, peer-reviewed, 13F-based crowding measure exists, which makes "validate the proxy against
  the holdings-based measure" a well-specified job rather than an undefined one. (ii) The newest work
  associates crowding with **prices, comovement and fragility** — *not* with a return-predictive
  trading rule — which is the same place the register's own bounded negative put it (S-172: crowding
  predicts tail risk rather than average returns; crowding-based factor selection fails out of sample).
- **Finding 2 — the two constructs are complements, not substitutes.** The literature treats a
  holdings-based crowding measure and a return-correlation crowding measure as *different objects with
  different information content*: the holdings-based measure is stale by construction (13F filings carry
  up to a quarter's reporting lag), while the return-correlation measure is contemporaneous but requires
  price history and assumes the correlation structure is informative about arbitrage positions. Nothing
  located in this cycle validates comomentum against the holdings-based measure. **G-18 therefore stands
  as a bounded negative, restated rather than closed** — the second consecutive cycle in which the
  register could not close it, which is itself the honest state of the question.
- **Effect on the dossier: no score change (69.0).** Mechanism 4/5 (the chain is intact, and the
  crowding state is now better documented as a construct); evidence 4/5 (the state's constructibility
  is *strengthened* by S-186, but the candidate's own two-sided conditioning still has no supporting
  empirical work); novelty 3/5 (unchanged — the differentiator is still the non-monotone *direction* of
  the conditioning, which the newest crowding work does not claim); falsifiability 3/5 (unchanged — the
  crowd proxy remains lagged and noisy); resilience 3/5 and data 3/5 (unchanged — the *validity* of the
  proxy, not its availability, is the binding constraint). **V-002 remains frozen** and must not be
  executed: re-specifying IDEA-004 around comomentum without a validation against the holdings-based
  measure would violate the register's state-proxy method.
- **What this cycle did *not* do.** It did not close G-18, did not re-score the candidate, and did not
  license a new proxy. The change is in the *description of the obstacle*: it is no longer "is a
  holdings-based crowding measure constructible?" but "does any published work validate a contemporaneous
  proxy against it, and does the two-sided conditioning survive in a modern sample in which crowding is
  associated with fragility rather than with average returns?"

### Cycle 35 (2026-10-20) — G-18 closed as a bounded negative that supersedes its own premise; the published crowding literature now corroborates the tail limb and makes the *state definition* the load-bearing question (G-04 restated, **G-28** opened)

- **The single most important new source.** S-222 (Chincarini, Lazo-Paz & Moneta 2025, "Crowded spaces and
  anomalies", *Journal of Banking & Finance* 182: 107579) relates **crowded trades** — "those in which
  many investors hold the same stocks possibly exhausting their liquidity provision" — to future returns
  on well-known stock-market anomalies, using 13F holdings over 1980–2021 and the **Days-ADV** crowding
  construct of Brown, Howard and Lundblad (2021), which is the register's own **S-026**. It finds that
  "anomaly risk-adjusted returns are primarily generated by most (least) crowded stocks for the
  long-leg (short-leg) portfolio", that the results survive publication-date controls, and hypothesises
  that crowded equity positions increase institutional exposure to crash risk, so that "crowding adds
  new considerations to limits to arbitrage".
- **What this does to G-18.** The registered G-18 question was whether comomentum (S-157) had been
  validated against a holdings-based crowding measure (S-026). That question is now **void**, and the
  reason is the finding: the literature never needed the cross-validation, because each construct has
  been validated against an **outcome** instead. The holdings-based construct is now an active published
  object with return-predictive results of its own, and the correlation-based construct (S-157) likewise
  carries published return-predictive results of its own. **G-18 is closed as a bounded negative** — no
  published work validates one crowding proxy against the other, and none is needed.
- **What this does to the candidate's own claim — three points, in the register's favour and against.**
  1. **The candidate may no longer be presented as discovering that crowding is a first-order
     conditioning variable.** That is now published (S-026, S-222). New hard-stop flag attached.
  2. **The published relation is *monotone* in crowding; the candidate's claim is a *two-sided
     (non-monotone)* gate.** The published result therefore does not support the candidate's
     two-sidedness, but it also does not kill it: the candidate's specific two-sided gate becomes the
     thing that must be pre-registered and tested, and the published monotone relation is a *prior* the
     candidate must be able to explain.
  3. **The published crowding population is a different crowd.** S-222 measures all 13F institutional
     investors holding the same stock — *directional positioning* — not the *convergence/arbitrage
     capital* the candidate's state is meant to capture, and it measures anomaly long/short portfolio
     returns rather than the payoff of a convergence trade. The candidate's tail limb (prediction 2: a
     worse left tail in high-crowd states) is the part most directly corroborated, since the published
     crash-risk channel and S-026's fragility result point the same way.
- **G-04 restated, and the new gap G-28 opened.** G-04's *proxy-validity* limb is no longer "validate
  comomentum against the holdings-based measure"; it is now: **the crowding state must be a
  position-concentration measure of the convergence/arbitrage crowd in the candidate's own universe**
  (pairs, merger-spread and index-convergence relative value), and the published neighbours (S-026's
  Days-ADV over all 13F institutions, S-222's anomaly-level crowding, S-157's comomentum) are **not**
  substitutes for it, because they measure a different crowd at a different frequency. Until that
  measure is specified, the candidate's rule cannot be written. This residual is registered as **G-28**.
- **Effect on V-002.** V-002 **remains frozen**, but the *reason* changes: it is no longer frozen
  pending a proxy validation that will never arrive, but pending **G-28** plus the Cycle-6 finding that
  the rule must be anchored to the 13F filing cadence. No re-specification is written while frozen.
- **Score: 69.0, unchanged.** Mechanism 4 → 4 (unchanged), evidence 4 → 4 (unchanged — the new source
  corroborates the family and the tail limb but does **not** support the two-sided conditioning, which
  remains the stated reason for not scoring 5), novelty 3 → 3 (unchanged), falsifiability 3 → 3
  (unchanged — the crowd proxy remains lagged and noisy, and the binding constraint is still the state
  *definition* rather than its availability), resilience 3 → 3 (unchanged), data 3 → 3 (unchanged).
  **Rank 2, unchanged** (tied with IDEA-011 at 69.0; the tie is broken in IDEA-011's favour on
  falsifiability, 4 versus 3).

#### Cycle 37 block — IDEA-004 · G-28 CONFIRMED open, and the register now says why in a way a validator can act on

- **What Cycle 37 did.** It did not search for a new candidate; it took the load-bearing gap on this
  dossier — **G-28, the convergence-crowd position-concentration measure** — and asked the question the
  bare verdict "not constructible from published data" left open: *is there a construction route, and
  if so why does it fail?* The verdict is unchanged (**open**), but the gap now names the route and
  the four reasons it is insufficient, so the next agent does not have to re-derive them.
- **The route that had not been named.** A position-concentration measure of the convergence crowd
  could in principle be **constructed from Form 13F holdings**: a manager disclosing long holdings in
  *both* legs of a pair, or in a merger-spread constituent and its acquirer, is by construction running
  convergence capital, and the disclosed holdings give the positions. The published crowding
  measures are *neighbours, not substitutes*: S-026's Days-ADV over hedge-fund holdings and S-222's
  crowding factor measure **all** 13F institutional investors — directional positioning — and S-157's
  comomentum measures arbitrage activity inferred from return correlations without identifying who is
  doing it.
- **Why the route still fails the register's bar.**
  1. **It is a construction, not a published measure.** The register's data rule requires a published
     state from a primary source. A register-built measure would have to be validated against an
     outcome before it could condition a rule, and validating it requires the outcome and the
     crowding state simultaneously.
  2. **It captures only the visible long leg.** Form 13F reports long Section 13(f) positions only —
     no shorts, no swaps, no credit instruments — so a convergence trade whose decisive leg is short
     is invisible, and a manager who has hedged a leg away is indistinguishable from one who never
     had it.
  3. **It cannot separate convergence capital from a coincident directional holder.** A manager
     holding both legs of a pair for reasons other than convergence is counted identically.
  4. **It cannot support the cadence the rule needs.** The register's already-established 45-day 13F
     filing lag makes the measure a quarter-slow, while IDEA-004's rule is re-anchored to its own
     cadence; a state that cannot be refreshed at the rule's frequency cannot condition it.
- **What would change the verdict.** A *published* measure of convergence-crowd position concentration
  at the candidate's own cadence, or a published validation of the holdings-matching construction
  against an outcome. The register must not substitute a published crowding measure (S-026, S-157,
  S-222) for the candidate's own crowd.
- **Effect on V-002.** **Remains frozen**, for the same reason and now with the reason written down.
  No re-specification is written while frozen.
- **Score: 69.0, unchanged. Rank 2, unchanged.** No new source bears on this candidate's score
  components, and the gap's status did not change.

### Cycle 40 block — G-28 restated: the FINRA short-interest route is a lead with objections, and the
### published-crowding inventory was incomplete (a correction to this dossier)

- **The route tested.** Cycle 40 tested the route the handoff named: FINRA's twice-monthly equity
  short-interest file (S-127, as extended in Cycle 40 with the data-catalogue page). The facts were
  fetched directly from FINRA: firms must report short positions in all customer and proprietary
  accounts in all equity securities **twice a month**, due by 6 p.m. ET on the second business day
  after the designated settlement date and published the following Tuesday (the 2026 schedule is
  published on the page); the published object is **per issue**, for all exchange-listed and OTC equity
  securities, with one rolling year online and archives by download, and **no firm-level breakdown**.
- **Why the route is a lead and not a resolution — three objections, and one of them is *not* the
  cadence objection.**
  1. **Granularity.** A per-issue aggregate across all reporting firms cannot identify the
     convergence crowd and cannot express its concentration; any mapping from per-issue short interest
     to the candidate's pairs is a register-built construction with the same defect as the Form 13F
     holdings-matching route, and it cannot separate convergence capital from a coincident directional
     short. Note that the *cadence* objection that usually kills a route does **not** apply here:
     twice-monthly publication with an ~8-business-day lag is **faster** than the 45-day 13F grid the
     candidate's rule is anchored to, so a validator must not reach for "too slow" here.
  2. **Contamination of the short leg.** In the classic pair trade the short leg is the recent winner,
     whose short interest also carries value, short-bias and index strategies; a pair-level
     aggregation would have to strip them out, and the published data cannot.
  3. **Prior art — and this is new in Cycle 40, and it corrects this dossier.** The published-neighbour
     inventory in this dossier listed only the 13F-based and correlation-based crowding measures
     (S-026's Days-ADV, S-157's comomentum, S-222's crowding factor). The **short-interest branch of
     the crowding literature exists and is peer-reviewed**: short interest is already a published
     crowding measure of arbitrage capital (Hanson & Sunderam, *RFS* 2014 — S-242), daily short interest
     has already been used to infer near-real-time crowding of quantitative long-short strategies (Chan
     & Tan, SSRN 2020 — S-243), and short interest is already the published measure of **merger-arb
     short selling** together with its price pressure (Mitchell, Pulvino & Stafford, *JF* 2004 —
     S-241). The candidate may therefore **not** present a short-interest-based measure of convergence
     activity as a discovery, and the register's inventory for this candidate was incomplete without
     it.
- **What the register's own baseline says this does *not* change.** The published short-interest
  crowding measures identify a **different crowd** — style arbitrage capital (value, momentum and other
  anomaly strategies) — and their published relation to outcomes is **monotone**, where this candidate
  claims a **two-sided** gate. So the additions sharpen the rediscovery guard; they do not close the
  candidate and do not publish its residual.
- **Effect on G-28 and V-002.** G-28 remains **open** and is restated with the FINRA route named as a
  third candidate construction alongside the 13F holdings-matching measure and the comomentum proxy,
  each with its objections written down. **V-002 remains frozen.** The unfreezing condition is
  unchanged: a *published* measure of convergence-crowd position concentration in the candidate's own
  universe at the candidate's cadence, or a published validation of a construction against an outcome.
- **Score: 69.0, unchanged. Rank 2, unchanged.** The three new sources bear on the rediscovery guard
  and on the completeness of the baseline, not on the candidate's score components.
- **Hard-stop flag added in Cycle 40.** The candidate may not present a short-interest-based measure
  of convergence-crowd activity — per issue or per pair — as an unpublished construction (S-241,
  S-242, S-243); a short-interest measure may be used only as a *published neighbour* that must be
  engaged, not as the candidate's own state.

#### Cycles 41–51 consolidated note — IDEA-004 · G-28 and V-002 both **frozen**, and no source bearing on this dossier
- Across Cycles 41–51 the register executed, in order: the family screens **R-021 … R-031** (Cycles 41–51;
  each a bounded negative on an obligation, a disclosure mandate, a benchmark methodology, a margin
  schedule, a capacity constraint, a tax/settlement mandate, an exchange fee schedule, a rating threshold,
  an IPO lock-up, an option expiry, an information-mandate deadline and a closing-price rule), the
  **Cycles 44–50** verdict backfill into the source ledger (Cycle 51), and the **G-29** execution and
  **G-30** exhaustion on IDEA-010. **None of these produced a source bearing on IDEA-004.** The dossier's
  published-neighbour inventory — S-026 (13F Days-ADV), S-157 (comomentum), S-222 (crowding factor),
  S-241/S-242/S-243 (the short-interest branch) — therefore **remains the complete inventory**, and
  G-28's unfreezing condition is **unchanged**: a *published* measure of convergence-crowd position
  concentration in the candidate's own universe at the candidate's cadence, or a published validation of a
  construction against an outcome.
- **Score: 69.0, unchanged. Rank 2, unchanged** (tied with IDEA-011 at 69.0 and behind it on
  falsifiability, 3 versus 4).

#### Cycle 52 block — IDEA-004 · a bounded re-check confirms the freeze, and the family screen added a *structural* reason that now also binds this candidate
- **What Cycle 52 did for this candidate.** A **bounded** re-check of G-28's unfreezing condition: did any
  publication appear in 2025–2026 that measures *convergence-crowd position concentration* — as distinct
  from style arbitrage capital — in a listed-equity universe at a twice-monthly or finer cadence? **No.**
  Every crowding measure the register holds (S-026, S-157, S-222, S-241, S-242, S-243) measures a
  **different crowd**, and the Cycle-52 corpus (S-252 … S-266) added none that does not. **G-28 stays
  frozen and V-002 stays frozen**, and the FINRA twice-monthly short-interest route remains a *lead with
  objections* (per-issue aggregate with no firm-level breakdown; contamination of the short leg; short
  interest already published prior art) rather than a resolution. No 13F filing and no FINRA file was
  re-run in Cycle 52.
- **The one thing Cycle 52 did add, and it is a *structural* addition to this dossier.** The R-032 family
  screen (venue-imposed trading halts, price bands and market-wide circuit breakers) surfaced the general
  structural fact that **a published, real-time state visible to every participant cannot be the source of
  an informational edge, and that a concession competed for inside a published window is competed away by
  the participants with the shortest latency** (S-260, S-263: the SIPs "republish the current price bands
  every 30 seconds"; the venue's Designated Sponsors are *obliged* to quote during the European call
  phase; the reopening is an auction). That is the **same structural reason** that closed the ex-dividend
  family (R-017), the payment-date family (R-012) and the closing-price family (R-031), and it bears on
  IDEA-004 in one specific way: **if a future published convergence-crowd measure turns out to be a
  by-product of a *published* venue or provider state that every participant can see in real time, then
  the candidate's edge would have to come from somewhere other than knowing it.** The candidate's own edge
  is *not* claimed to be informational — it is claimed to be a **capacity-conditional** limitation of
  arbitrage capital (SLOWCAP-COND) — so this does not close the candidate. But it does sharpen what a
  future unfreezing of G-28 would have to show: not merely that a published measure exists, but that the
  measure is **not** a public real-time by-product of the crowd's own trading.
- **Score: 69.0, unchanged. Rank 2, unchanged.** No source bearing on any score component was found; the
  Cycle-52 corpus bears on the candidate's *rediscovery guard*, not on its mechanism, evidence, novelty,
  falsifiability, resilience or data.

#### Cycle 58 block — IDEA-004 · **G-04 closed on the proxy-validity limb**, and G-28's freeze acquires a **structural** reason rather than a search-based one
- **What Cycle 58 ran.** The **G-04** proxy-validity kill condition was executed as a check, not a
  re-declaration. The question was whether the crowding proxy the candidate would use is **valid against a
  published outcome**. The answer is **yes**, and the answer retires a question the register had been
  carrying for many cycles: **each crowding construct the register holds has already been validated
  against a published outcome** — the holdings-based Days-ADV crowding construct against crash and tail
  risk (S-026, S-222), the correlation-based comomentum construct against arbitrage activity and returns
  (S-157), and, newly registered this cycle, the position-based arbitrage-activity measures (S-291:
  abnormal hedge-fund holdings, abnormal short interest and their net combination), the short-interest
  arbitrage-capital measure (S-242) and the ownership-dispersion construct (S-296). **The
  comomentum-versus-holdings cross-validation the register had been demanding was therefore never
  needed** — each construct had been validated against an *outcome* from the start. **G-04's validity limb
  is closed in the candidate's favour.**
- **What remains is the *identity* limb, and it is registered as G-28 — now frozen for a structural
  reason.** The candidate's state must be a position-concentration measure of **the convergence crowd in
  the candidate's own universe**, and no published measure of that crowd was located. Cycle 58's
  contribution is to say *why*, in a way that does not depend on how hard anyone searches: every published
  crowding, arbitrage-activity and concentration construct the register now holds is **security-level and
  single-sided** — abnormal hedge-fund holdings and abnormal short interest (S-291), short-interest-implied
  arbitrage capital (S-242), ownership dispersion (S-296), the holdings-based Days-ADV measure (S-026,
  S-222) and correlation-implied comomentum (S-157) — because each is built from *disclosed aggregate
  positions in securities*. **A convergence crowd is defined by a pair of positions**: long one leg, short
  the other. A single-sided, security-level measure cannot represent it, because the same disclosed long
  holding may be a convergence leg or a directional bet, and the short leg is either invisible or
  contaminated by unrelated shorting. The absence is a property of the available position-disclosure
  sources, not of the register's searching — a stronger and more durable null than "nothing was found".
- **Consequences, all of which are constraints rather than changes.** (i) IDEA-004 stays at **69.0,
  rank 2**. (ii) **V-002 stays frozen**, now for a sharper reason: the unfreezing condition is not "a
  published measure exists" but "a published measure *of a paired, netted, security-pair crowd* exists",
  which no position-disclosure source can supply. (iii) The standing prohibition is reaffirmed: the
  candidate **may not substitute** any published crowding proxy (S-026, S-157, S-222, S-242, S-291, S-296),
  because each measures a different crowd. (iv) No new gap is opened on G-28; it remains open and frozen
  with a dated re-check condition.
- **New hard-stop flag — added.** A candidate in this family may **not** argue that the convergence crowd
  is unmeasured merely because no published measure was found. The register now holds a **structural**
  reason: the published position-disclosure sources are security-level and single-sided, and a convergence
  crowd is a *pair* of positions. A future unfreezing must therefore supply a measure that is itself
  pair-level and netted, or a published *validation of a construction* against an outcome — and, per the
  Cycle-52 addition, one that is not a public real-time by-product of the crowd's own trading.
- **Score: 69.0, unchanged. Rank 2, unchanged.** No source bearing on any score component was found. The
  Cycle-58 sources bear on the candidate's *state specification* — they decide which gap binds — not on
  its mechanism, evidence, novelty, falsifiability, resilience or data. The Cycle-35/52 precedent applies:
  a source that narrows the specification without supporting the candidate's directional claim does not
  move a score.

#### Cycle 59 block — IDEA-004 · **G-28's bounded re-check executed against the structural question**; the null is now established by the *structure of the available source classes*, not by the register's searching
- **What Cycle 59 ran, and what it did not run.** The Cycle-58 handoff asked for **one bounded re-check
  against the structural reason**, and explicitly warned against re-running the old, search-based question
  ("is there a published crowding measure"). Cycle 59 did exactly that: it asked whether any published
  source is **pair-level and netted** — that is, one that publishes a participant's longs *and* shorts, or
  a net position, for a defined participant class, at a granularity at which a two-legged convergence crowd
  is visible. **No such source was found**, and the classes that were checked explain why: 13F is
  **long-only** and aggregated without pair-level netting; 13D/13G are **beneficial-ownership**
  disclosures, not systematic pair-level crowd disclosure; FINRA short interest is an **issue-level
  aggregate** with no firm-level breakdown (the Cycle-40 objection, unchanged); CFTC category tables are
  **aggregates**. Every published position-disclosure source is therefore **security-level and
  single-sided**, and a convergence crowd is a **pair** of positions.
- **What this changes and what it does not.** The null is **unchanged in substance and strengthened in
  status**: it is no longer "no published measure was located" but "**no published measure of this kind
  can exist in the available source classes**", which is a statement about the world rather than about the
  register's effort. Consequently: (i) **G-28 remains open and frozen**; (ii) **V-002 remains frozen**; and
  (iii) the **unfreezing condition is unchanged and now final in wording** — a published **pair-level,
  netted** measure of the convergence crowd's position concentration, or a published *validation of a
  construction* against an outcome, that is not a public real-time by-product of the crowd's own trading.
  A future agent must **not** re-run the crowding-measure search; it must either supply a source of the
  pair-level kind or accept the freeze.
- **Standing prohibitions reaffirmed.** The candidate may **not** substitute any published crowding proxy
  (S-026, S-157, S-222, S-242, S-291, S-296), because each measures a different crowd; and the Cycle-58
  hard-stop flag stands — the candidate may **not** argue that the convergence crowd is unmeasured merely
  because no published measure was found.
- **Score: 69.0, unchanged. Rank 2, unchanged.** Cycle 59 consumed **no `S-` identifier**: the re-check
  produced a negative about a *class* of sources and therefore required no new citation. Nothing found
  bears on any score component; the Cycle-35/52 precedent applies.

## IDEA-005 — ROLLCOND: the scheduled commodity-index roll as a size-conditional liquidity demand, and the state that decides whether the concession survives

- **ID / short name:** IDEA-005 / ROLLCOND
- **Date added:** 2026-10-19 · **Cycle:** 2 · **Status:** **parked (P-009)** · **Version:** 1.0
- **Change notes:** v1.0 — first dossier. The underlying event (a scheduled passive roll) is
  documented, and the *headline* trade (front-running the roll) is documented as having decayed.
  The candidate is the **state-conditioned, provider-side** specification: the concession is
  predicted to survive only where the passive share of open interest is high and depth is thin.

**One-sentence hypothesis.** Commodity index trackers must move a long futures position from the
expiring contract into the deferred contract on a published calendar; because that demand is
price-insensitive, dated and knowable in advance, it buys a liquidity concession that a
*scheduled* provider can earn — but the concession exists only where the passive flow is large
relative to the contract's liquidity, a condition that is publicly observable and that has become
the binding constraint since electronification.

**Plain-language description.** Every month, on days the index provider has published years in
advance, a large pile of passive money has to move from one futures contract to the next. It does
not care what the price is; it has to be done. Whoever is standing on the other side of that
order gets a better price than the fundamentals justify, and the passive pays for it in
transaction costs. But the concession is only worth anything where the passive is a large share of
the market and the market is thin — in the deep, heavily arbitraged contracts, other dealers
supply that liquidity faster than the passive arrives, and the premium has disappeared.

**Mechanism (causal chain).**
1. Passive commodity exposure is obtained by holding long futures positions and rolling them; the
   index provider publishes the contract schedule and the roll schedule, and the S&P GSCI rolls
   monthly from the **5th to the 9th business day**, in fixed proportions 80/20 → 60/40 → 40/60 →
   20/80 → 0/100, with a documented procedure for holding roll weights if a market disruption
   event occurs (S-029). The clock is public and stable.
2. The regulator names the participant and the constraint: the CFTC's own explanatory notes define
   the "Index Traders" category in the Supplemental Commitments of Traders report as traders
   "replicating a commodity index by establishing long futures positions in the component markets
   and then rolling those positions forward from future to future using a fixed methodology"
   (S-030). The mechanism is documented by the regulator, not inferred from a price pattern.
3. The passive flow is not price-sensitive — exposure must be maintained whatever the price — so
   the price must move to ration a finite liquidity supply. The concession is the compensation
   paid to whoever supplies that liquidity, and it is a *cost* to the passive rather than a
   transfer from an informed counterparty.
4. The economically correct expression of "supply liquidity to a predictable large trade" is
   supplied *before* the trade, on a schedule, not raced against other speculators.
   Bessembinder, Carrion, Tuttle and Venkataraman (2016) model strategic trading around a
   predictable liquidation and find that competition among such traders improves market quality
   and increases the liquidator's proceeds (S-032) — the channel that the later literature labels
   "sunshine trading" (S-034). In their USO evidence the price imbalance around the roll "is
   almost entirely reversed within one minute in the expiring contract and within three minutes in
   the second nearest-to-expiration contract on roll days", and the passive fund pays real money
   for the privilege — they estimate roll trading costs averaging about three percent per year
   (S-032).
5. The size of the concession must scale with the passive flow relative to the *available
   liquidity* in the affected contract, and the relevant components are publicly observable:
   the passive's own position in the expiring contract (CFTC "Index Traders" positions, weekly,
   positions as of Tuesday, published Friday), the arbitrage/liquidity-supply state (managed money
   and swap dealer positions in the Disaggregated COT report), the contract's open interest,
   volume and depth, and the curve (whether the roll is into a more or less expensive deferred
   contract).
6. The state is not constant, and its evolution is the reason the published literature disagrees.
   Mou (2011) found the front-running profit depends on the size of index investment net of the
   arbitrage capital employed (S-035); Stoll & Whaley (2010) found index rolls have little futures
   price impact (S-036); Hamilton & Wu (2015) found that index-trader positions do not predict
   near-contract excess returns and that the oil relation breaks down out of sample (S-037); Yan,
   Irwin & Sanders (2022) find the impact of predictable rebalancing flows to be "modest and
   temporary" (S-034); and Irwin, Sanders & Yan (2023) find that the roll's impact on the
   nearby-versus-deferred spread **disappears in 2012–2019**, attributing this to the increase in
   liquidity supply from electronification (S-033).
7. These results are not inconsistent — they are measurements of an average dominated by the
   deepest contracts, in a period when the marginal liquidity supplier was a human on a floor. The
   candidate's claim is a *cross-sectional* one: the concession survives in the contracts where
   the passive share of open interest is high and the liquidity supply is thin, and vanishes in
   the deep ones. That is a prediction the aggregate time-series studies are not powered to see,
   and it is contradicted if the cross-section is flat.

**Expected return source.** Compensation for supplying liquidity to a dated, price-insensitive
demand shock — a *liquidity-provision premium* paid by the passive — collected by a participant
who is scheduled to be present rather than one who has to guess. The premium is bounded by the
passive's own execution cost (documented at roughly three percent per year for one large ETF's
roll in S-032), which is the ceiling on what can be harvested and also the reason the residual
after costs is hard to establish.

**Originality.**
- *Closest known work:* Mou (2011) "Front-Running the Goldman Roll" (S-035); Stoll & Whaley
  (2010) (S-036); Hamilton & Wu (2015) (S-037); Bessembinder, Carrion, Tuttle & Venkataraman
  (2016) (S-032); Yan, Irwin & Sanders (2022) (S-034); Irwin, Sanders & Yan (2023) (S-033);
  Tang & Xiong (2012) on index investment and the financialisation of commodities; and the
  inventory-based risk-premium literature (S-038, S-039).
- *What is already known:* that the passive exists, rolls on a published schedule, and is large
  in some contracts; that the roll moved the nearby-deferred spread before 2012 and that this
  reversed after the roll completed; that the impact in the deepest contract is transient and
  that the passive's execution cost is large; that the aggregate effect disappeared after 2012;
  and that index-trader positions do not predict near-contract excess returns.
- *Substantive differentiator claimed here:* (i) the **state** — the passive flow relative to
  liquidity, read directly from the regulator's own Index Trader positions — as a first-class
  conditioning variable, with a *sign* prediction (survival only in high-passive-share, thin
  contracts) that aggregate studies cannot produce; (ii) the **provider-side** expression rather
  than the front-runner-side one, which is the expression consistent with the "sunshine trading"
  evidence and with the documented decay of the front-running effect; and (iii) the
  **reconciling** claim: the published null is a composition effect of deep contracts, not a
  statement that the mechanism is dead everywhere.
- *Honest classification:* **documented and contested effect family; the candidate is a
  state-conditioned, provider-side refinement.** This is not a new effect and must never be
  presented as one. Its value is that the *state* is public, the *dates* are public, and the
  family has a documented, peer-reviewed null that the candidate must survive.
- *Gap G-06 result (Cycle 3):* the commodity basis-convergence literature was located
  (S-032 … S-039) and establishes that the roll concession, as an aggregate post-2012 fact, is
  dead (S-033), transient (S-032, S-034), and not predicted by positions (S-036, S-037). It does
  **not** condition the roll-period nearby-to-deferred spread concession on the passive share of
  open interest × contract depth, and it does not establish the concession as a plain
  convergence-to-carry trade with a calendar catalyst rather than a liquidity-provision concession
  around a scheduled roll. G-06 is therefore **narrowed, not closed**: the claim that "nobody has
  conditioned the roll impact on the passive share of open interest" survives as a conjecture,
  not an exhaustive negative finding, and must be re-visited before IDEA-005 is presented as a
  discovery.
- *Confidence in the comparison:* moderate-to-high. The closest work (S-035) and the three
  peer-reviewed studies that contradict it (S-032, S-033, S-034) were read at the level of
  abstracts, publisher records and the CFTC-hosted full text of S-035; S-032 was read in the
  author-hosted PDF.

**Target scope.** US-traded commodity futures (CME, CBOT, NYMEX, ICE US) that are components of
passive commodity benchmarks; the first-nearby (expiring) and second-nearby (deferred) contracts;
holding horizon intraday to a few sessions around each monthly roll; per commodity and per
contract; regime conditions: not in delivery months, not when a price limit or a market
disruption event suspends the roll (the provider's own roll-weight adjustment rule applies),
and not in a contract whose liquidity has collapsed in the roll week.

**Observables (knowable at decision time, conceptually).**
- The published roll calendar, per-day roll weights and the market-disruption adjustment rule
  (S-029) — known years ahead, so no look-ahead.
- The CFTC Supplemental "Index Traders" position in the expiring contract, from the report
  published on Friday for positions as of the prior Tuesday (S-030, S-031) — available with a
  known five-day lag, and applied to a *scheduled* future date, so the lag is not a look-ahead.
- Managed money and swap dealer positions for the same contracts (Disaggregated COT), as the
  arbitrage/liquidity-supply state (S-030, S-031).
- Contract open interest, traded volume, quoted spread, depth, and the size of the passive flow
  implied by the index weight change and index scale.
- The nearby-to-deferred price spread and the cost-of-carry bound it is measured against.
- *Timing hazards to flag for the validator:* weekly COT data with a five-day publication lag
  (a decision taken on a roll day uses the previous Friday's report); the roll can be extended or
  held by a market disruption event (S-029); contract specifications and index methodologies
  change (the S&P GSCI Enhanced family uses a different, partly dynamic roll schedule, S-029);
  delivery months and first notice days distort the expiring contract; and the COT "Index Traders"
  category itself contains classification error and includes some non-index activity (S-030).

**Rule sketch (descriptive, non-executable).** For each component commodity of the passive
benchmark universe, on each roll day: compute the passive-flow-to-liquidity ratio from the most
recent published Index Trader position in the expiring contract divided by that contract's open
interest; screen to contracts where the ratio is high and the contract's depth is small relative
to the expected roll notional; take the position on the side *opposite* the passive's roll — that
is, supply the liquidity the passive requires — with the size bounded by a fraction of the
expected roll notional and by the contract's depth; exit on a pre-specified schedule tied to the
completion of the roll, with the exit window defined in advance. Exclusions: delivery months,
contracts under a price limit, contracts in which the passive share is small, and any contract
where the liquidity in the roll window has visibly collapsed.

**Persistence thesis.** The passive's need to trade is a mandate, not a view, and it is renewed
every month, so the demand recurs; a provider who can be *scheduled* rather than reactive keeps a
structural edge over a front-runner who must locate the flow in real time. The premium persists
where the alternative liquidity supply is thin. It decays — and has decayed, on the published
evidence — where liquidity supply becomes elastic (electronification, S-033) or where arbitrage
capital pre-positions the roll in advance (the alternative explanation for the post-2012 null).
Both decay channels are observable, which is the point of the state variable.

**Constraints.** Cost/liquidity: thin contracts have wide spreads, and the passive's own execution
cost (~3% per year in the USO case, S-032) bounds the harvestable premium; the strategy consumes
exactly the liquidity it is trying to price. Capacity: per contract and per roll the capacity is
bounded by the passive flow actually present, so it scales with passive assets, not with the
strategy's own balance sheet. Financing/margin: futures require margin, and a position that is
short the deferred contract carries margin that moves with volatility. Market impact: taking the
opposite side of a large scheduled flow in a thin contract is an impact machine. Venue/operational:
exchange position limits, delivery rules and price limits; a market disruption event can change
the roll schedule mid-flow (S-029). Shorting constraints: not a borrow-constrained trade, but
futures shorting is margin- and limit-constrained, and in a thin contract the position can be
expensive to exit exactly when it must be unwound.

**Regime sensitivity, tail exposure, crash behavior, hidden beta.** Regime: entirely state-
dependent by construction — in deep, liquid, heavily arbitraged contracts the candidate predicts
no edge at all, which is the majority of contracts by notional. Tail: the position is short one
leg of a curve; a sudden supply shock (a real production failure, a policy change, a war) moves
the whole curve, and the position is short the deferred contract into a rising price. Crash
behaviour: in a liquidity crisis the thin contracts are the least liquid and the position is
hardest to exit; the passive's own roll is also least well executed then, which cuts both ways.
Hidden beta: the position is short a deferred commodity contract, i.e. it is a short position in
the "carry" component of commodity returns, and will load on the momentum/carry factors and on
the dollar. Correlated risks: every commodity's roll falls in the same five days of the month, so
the book is a single month-end-dated bet on the passive complex; a single contract blow-up
dominates many small contract-level results.

**Falsifiable predictions and rejection conditions.**
1. *Cross-sectional survival:* the roll-period price concession (the deviation of the
   nearby-to-deferred spread from its no-arbitrage carry bound) is larger in contracts with a
   high passive share of open interest than in contracts with a low passive share, in the same
   period and the same commodity. **Kill:** the relation is flat or reversed across contracts.
2. *Liquidity condition:* the concession is larger in the thinnest contracts (measured by depth or
   by the passive flow relative to open interest) and negligible in the deepest. **Kill:** the
   concession is proportional to the passive flow but not to liquidity, or is present in the deep
   contracts too.
3. *Post-roll reversal:* the deviation reverses after the roll completes, and the reversal is
   larger the larger the deviation was. **Kill:** no reversal, or a reversal fully explained by
   the carry bound moving (a change in storage/financing costs) rather than by the flow.
4. *Modern-period survival:* the effect is not absent in 2012–present in the thin, high-passive-
   share contracts, notwithstanding the published aggregate null (S-033). **Kill:** the effect is
   absent in every contract in the modern period — in which case the candidate is dead and the
   published null is a statement about the whole market, not a composition effect.
5. *Discriminating the decay channel:* if the decay is due to *arbitrage capital pre-positioning*,
   the concession should be smallest in contracts where managed money positioning in the deferred
   contract is already high before the roll; if it is due to *electronification and liquidity
   supply*, the concession should be smallest in contracts with the deepest order books and
   unchanged in thin contracts with little pre-positioning. **Kill:** neither pattern holds.
6. *Cost floor:* the realised concession per contract exceeds the round-trip cost of the trade
   (spread, commission, slippage, margin financing) in the contracts the rule selects.
   **Kill:** the gross concession is smaller than the cost in every selected contract.

**Main confounders and rival explanations.**
- *Electronification and liquidity supply* (S-033) — the leading rival: the effect may be gone
  everywhere, and the cross-section may be a story about measurement rather than about markets.
- *Arbitrage capital pre-positioning* — the roll may be pre-absorbed by spread traders, so there
  is nothing to supply; Mou (2011) found profitability depends on the balance of index
  investment against arbitrage capital (S-035), which is a state, not a constant.
- *Fundamental news* — commodity fundamentals move on the same calendar as the roll (USDA, EIA,
  OPEC, weather); a spread move that coincides with a fundamental repricing is not a liquidity
  concession.
- *Carry and storage economics* — the nearby-deferred spread is driven by convenience yield and
  storage costs, which are themselves state-dependent (S-038); a "deviation from the carry bound"
  must be measured against a *state-dependent* bound, not a constant.
- *Hedging pressure* — producer hedging is a separate, documented driver of futures risk premia
  (S-039) and can move the same spread for the same calendar reasons.
- *Classification error in the COT data* — the Index Traders category contains non-index activity
  and excludes some index traders (S-030), and it is reported at the trader level with a weekly
  lag.
- *The candidate may be a dressed-up basis-convergence trade* — the commodity basis-convergence
  literature was located in Cycle 3 (S-032 … S-039, gap G-06 narrowed but not closed). If the
  concession is simply a convergence-to-carry trade with a calendar catalyst, it is a variant of a
  known baseline and must be reclassified.

**Data requirements and feasibility for a future independent validation.** (Specification only —
nothing is acquired or processed here.) Public: futures settlement and trade prices by contract
month, open interest and volume (exchange data); CFTC Supplemental CIT and Disaggregated COT
reports (S-030, S-031); the index's contract weights, roll schedule and index scale (S-029, and
a passive-asset scale series — see G-07). Feasibility: **medium-high** for the cross-sectional
event study (all inputs are public and the calendar is fixed); **medium** for the state
construction (COT classification error and a five-day lag); **medium** for the cost model (thin
contract fills are the binding assumption and must be justified independently).

**Theory/evidence status.**
- *Conceptual rationale:* supported — price pressure from non-price-sensitive scheduled demand
  (S-038, S-039) and the economics of liquidity provision around predictable large trades (S-032).
- *Mechanism supported by sources:* **yes** for the participant, the constraint and the dated
  schedule — the regulator documents the Index Trader roll methodology (S-030) and the provider
  documents the roll calendar (S-029).
- *Prior empirical support reported by others:* mixed and contested. Support for a pre-2012
  effect: S-035 (working paper, non-refereed). Support for transience and high costs: S-032.
  Support for modest and temporary impact: S-034. Evidence of an aggregate null post-2012: S-033.
  Evidence that index-trader positions do not predict near-contract excess returns: S-037 and
  S-036. None of these is reproduced here.
- *Unresolved:* whether any cross-sectional residual survives in the modern period, and which
  decay channel (liquidity supply or pre-positioning) is responsible for the published null.

**Remaining uncertainty / confidence.** Confidence that the participant, the constraint and the
schedule are real and publicly dated: **high** (regulator and provider documentation).
Confidence that a *harvestable* concession survives today: **low-to-moderate** — three
independent peer-reviewed lines point the other way for the aggregate and for the deep contract.
Confidence in the state-conditioning as the right way to resolve the disagreement: **moderate** —
it is the only formulation found that both (a) uses a public variable and (b) predicts a
*sign* change rather than a smaller magnitude. Priority score **72/100**; rank 3 of 7 at the time
of writing. **Concrete next question:** is the roll-period concession in the nearby-to-deferred
spread a function of the passive share of open interest and of contract depth, and does it survive
in 2012–present in the thin, high-passive-share contracts where the published aggregate null
(S-033) says it should not exist?

**Cycle 6 screen (G-13 publication timing; G-12 sign or existence). — AND PARKING.**
- **G-13 — the state is published before the decision: PASS.** The COT reports are published
  (S-030, S-031) on a weekly cadence ahead of the roll window, and the roll calendar is fixed by
  the index provider (S-029). There is no publication-timing defect.
- **G-12 — the conditioning changes the sign or the existence of the effect: FAIL, on the
  available evidence.** The candidate's own formulation is a *state-conditioned existence* claim
  ("the concession survives only where passive share is high and depth is thin"), and on its face
  that is an existence claim, not a size claim. But G-12 is a screen on what the candidate
  *demonstrates*, not on how it is worded, and here the demonstration runs the other way: the
  closest published test of the candidate's **own state variable** — the CFTC Index Traders' share
  of open interest, conditioned on *commodity returns during roll periods* — reports **no
  significant effect** (S-099), and the published work on the trader-type channel shows the
  conditioning running on *rate of convergence* rather than on the nearby-to-deferred concession
  (S-098). G-06 closed as a **bounded negative finding**: no located paper conditions the joint
  state (passive share × depth) on the spread concession. So the joint claim is untested, its
  nearest partial test is null, and the **aggregate** effect the joint claim is supposed to rescue
  is itself documented as decayed: the roll's impact on the nearby-versus-deferred spread
  "disappears in the 2012–2019 subperiod" (S-033), with index-trader positions not predicting
  near-contract excess returns (S-037, S-036).
- **Parking decision (P-009), and why it is a parking and not a rejection.** IDEA-005 is **not** a
  re-description of published work — no located paper claims what it claims, and the register's
  rule "reject rather than narrow" applies when a published result *shows the candidate's effect*,
  which is not the case here (S-099 finds a null, not the effect). But it is also no longer
  *actionable*: its own state's closest published test is null, the effect it would condition has
  been documented as decayed in the modern sample, and the joint state requires contract depth,
  which is real-time observable but is not a published, downloadable series — so the candidate
  would have to be built on an unpublished input to be tested at all. Parking preserves the
  falsifiable question and the two unblocking conditions, and stops the candidate being presented
  as a discovery.
- **Score: 72.0 → 60.0.** Evidence **3 → 2** (the closest published test of its own state variable
  is null — S-099 — and the aggregate result is null post-2012 — S-033, S-037, S-036). Novelty
  **3 → 2** (the joint state is unclaimed, but the claim now rests on a null rather than on an
  open question). Falsifiability **4 → 3** (a modern test would face a likely-null base effect, and
  the joint state needs a non-published depth input). Data **5 → 4** (the COT component is
  published; contract depth is real-time observable but not a published downloadable series).
  Mechanism 4 and resilience 3 unchanged.
- **Unblocking conditions, both required.** (i) A published or constructible test of the **joint**
  state — passive share **×** depth — against the nearby-to-deferred spread concession, which would
  either revive or close the candidate; and (ii) evidence that the concession **survives
  2012–present** in the thin, high-passive-share contracts, which is the specific reconciliation
  the candidate offers against S-033. Until both are met, IDEA-005 must not be presented as a
  candidate, must not be given a validation specification, and must not be re-specified around a
  weaker proxy.

---
